E Conversations

Tidy is not confirmed ready

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Maya: Receptionist / Owen: Nail technician

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • read-back
  • completion time
  • confirmed ready
  • available alternative
  • Pending
  • release confirmation
  • readiness handover
  • readiness check
  • tidy
  • responsible technician
  1. Maya
    Can I seat the waiting client at station three? The table looks clear from here.
  2. Owen
    Not yet. It's 1, but the readiness checks aren't complete. Station two is the one Rosa has confirmed ready.
  3. Maya
    Two, not three. Thanks for catching that. I'll move the client to two rather than leave them standing at reception.
  4. Owen
    Yes, that's the 2. Three needs to stay out of use until the remaining checks are finished and confirmed.
  5. Maya
    The board says almost ready beside three. Should that say pending instead?
  6. Owen
    Yes. 3 makes the status clearer. Almost ready could encourage someone else to seat a client there.
  7. Maya
    I'll change the label. Do you know how long the remaining checks will take?
  8. Owen
    I don't have a confirmed 4 yet. Please don't promise the next client five minutes on that basis.
  9. Maya
    All right. For the client who's here, I'll say we have another station available now.
  10. Owen
    That works. Station two is 5; we don't need to give the client a detailed account of the unfinished checks elsewhere.
  11. Maya
    Who confirmed two? I'd like to put the name in the note so the next receptionist can see it.
  12. Owen
    Rosa is the 6. She's already given that confirmation, so you can name her.
  13. Maya
    Good. Does a cleared work surface at three tell us anything about the tools that will be used there?
  14. Owen
    Not by itself. The 7 follows our actual process. An empty table doesn't establish that the tools or other requirements are ready.
  15. Maya
    Then I'll leave the station pending, even if the client says they're happy to sit there.
  16. Owen
    Correct. We still need the 8 after the checks. Their willingness doesn't complete our responsibilities.
  17. Maya
    Let me repeat it: two ready, confirmed by Rosa; three pending, no confirmed time. Is that everything?
  18. Owen
    That's an accurate 9. It should prevent the ready label ending up beside the wrong station.
  19. Maya
    I'll offer two now and wait for an actual update before changing three on the board.
  20. Owen
    Thanks. Keep that 10 for the next receptionist too, so nobody has to guess from what the room looks like.

Answers and explanations
  1. tidy Turn 2. Tidy describes appearance only; station three still has incomplete required readiness checks.
  2. available alternative Turn 4. Station two is the verified alternative; using it does not change station three's unfinished status.
  3. Pending Turn 6. Pending clearly indicates unfinished checks, whereas almost ready can be mistaken for permission to start.
  4. completion time Turn 8. No reliable completion time is supplied for station three, so a waiting estimate would be invented.
  5. confirmed ready Turn 10. Rosa has confirmed station two through the salon process; that status cannot be transferred to station three.
  6. responsible technician Turn 12. Rosa is the named source of the station-two confirmation, avoiding an anonymous or assumed approval.
  7. readiness check Turn 14. The readiness check must cover the actual required items; surface appearance alone does not verify tools or other controls.
  8. release confirmation Turn 16. Release confirmation is the actual readiness decision after the required checks, not the client's willingness to use the station.
  9. read-back Turn 18. The read-back connects the correct station number with its status, confirmation source, and timing limit.
  10. readiness handover Turn 20. The handover gives an immediately usable option while retaining the pending station's actual status.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Maya: Can I seat the waiting client at station three? The table looks clear from here.
  2. Owen: Not yet. It's tidy, but the readiness checks aren't complete. Station two is the one Rosa has confirmed ready.
  3. Maya: Two, not three. Thanks for catching that. I'll move the client to two rather than leave them standing at reception.
  4. Owen: Yes, that's the available alternative. Three needs to stay out of use until the remaining checks are finished and confirmed.
  5. Maya: The board says almost ready beside three. Should that say pending instead?
  6. Owen: Yes. Pending makes the status clearer. Almost ready could encourage someone else to seat a client there.
  7. Maya: I'll change the label. Do you know how long the remaining checks will take?
  8. Owen: I don't have a confirmed completion time yet. Please don't promise the next client five minutes on that basis.
  9. Maya: All right. For the client who's here, I'll say we have another station available now.
  10. Owen: That works. Station two is confirmed ready; we don't need to give the client a detailed account of the unfinished checks elsewhere.
  11. Maya: Who confirmed two? I'd like to put the name in the note so the next receptionist can see it.
  12. Owen: Rosa is the responsible technician. She's already given that confirmation, so you can name her.
  13. Maya: Good. Does a cleared work surface at three tell us anything about the tools that will be used there?
  14. Owen: Not by itself. The readiness check follows our actual process. An empty table doesn't establish that the tools or other requirements are ready.
  15. Maya: Then I'll leave the station pending, even if the client says they're happy to sit there.
  16. Owen: Correct. We still need the release confirmation after the checks. Their willingness doesn't complete our responsibilities.
  17. Maya: Let me repeat it: two ready, confirmed by Rosa; three pending, no confirmed time. Is that everything?
  18. Owen: That's an accurate read-back. It should prevent the ready label ending up beside the wrong station.
  19. Maya: I'll offer two now and wait for an actual update before changing three on the board.
  20. Owen: Thanks. Keep that readiness handover for the next receptionist too, so nobody has to guess from what the room looks like.
Dialogue in the book, page 55 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Tidy does not mean ready

A technician gives a waiting client a station update.

  1. Technician: Station three is tidy, but its required readiness checks haven't been completed yet.
  2. Client: It looks empty and clean from here. Is that different from being ready for my appointment?
  3. Technician: Yes. Appearance alone doesn't confirm those checks. Station two has been confirmed ready.
  4. Client: I'd prefer the window station, but I don't want to wait without an estimate.
  5. Technician: I don't have a reliable wait for station three; I can offer station two now.
  6. Client: Would changing stations also change the technician or the service we already agreed on?
  7. Technician: No. The same technician and agreed service would use the confirmed space at station two.
  8. Client: Then I'll take that option. Please don't rush the other station just for my preference.
  9. Technician: Thank you. I'll direct you to station two and leave station three's status unchanged.
  10. Client: That works. Knowing which space is actually ready matters more than the window today.
A colleague needs the status, not a procedure

Two technicians clarify an incomplete station note.

  1. Technician: The station note says cleared, but reception is interpreting that word as ready for the next client.
  2. Lead technician: Cleared means used items were removed; it doesn't record completion of all the required preparation.
  3. Technician: Then I'll correct reception's understanding. Which station can they offer the waiting client right now?
  4. Lead technician: Station one is confirmed ready. Station four is still awaiting its readiness check this afternoon.
  5. Technician: I'll give them that distinction without estimating how long the remaining check will take.
  6. Lead technician: Please also avoid saying disinfection is finished merely because the work surface looks tidy.
  7. Technician: Please let reception know when station four is ready. They've got another client arriving at three.
  8. Lead technician: I will. We're still checking the disinfection record, including the required contact time for these implements.
  9. Technician: I'll send the waiting client to station one and keep station four marked unavailable.
  10. Lead technician: Thanks. I'll contact reception directly once we've finished the remaining checks at four.
A label question deserves a specific answer

A client asks about a packaged item before a service.

  1. Client: Is this item reused after each customer, or is it meant for one use?
  2. Technician: The package identifies this particular item as single-use. Reusable implements are a different category.
  3. Client: I saw several similar-looking items nearby, so I wasn't sure whether appearance told me anything.
  4. Technician: Similar appearance doesn't establish the item's category or preparation status. We check the actual item.
  5. Client: Could you explain that distinction without giving me a long technical description of the chemicals?
  6. Technician: Certainly. Single-use means intended for one use; reusable means it has preparation requirements before reuse.
  7. Client: So reusable doesn't mean it is automatically ready just because it can be used again?
  8. Technician: Correct. Its permitted reuse and its present readiness are different questions to check.
  9. Client: Thanks, I wanted to check before we started. I'd rather ask now than worry during the appointment.
  10. Technician: Of course. This item is single-use, and I'll confirm the other items are ready before we begin.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Tidy is not confirmed ready
  1. Read the station exchange, clearly contrasting two ready with three pending.
  2. Switch roles. Read the no-confirmed-time response without adding a five-minute estimate.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Read back different station numbers

Station five is tidy but has incomplete required checks. Station one is confirmed ready by technician Jo. No completion time is known for five. A waiting client can be offered one.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Reception: "The verified alternative is station ___."

Answer and explanation

C. one

Jo has confirmed station one ready, making it the available option.

2. Technician: "Station five remains ___."

Answer and explanation

B. pending

Its required checks are incomplete despite the tidy appearance.

3. Reception: "The confirming technician for one is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. Jo

Jo is the named source of the station-one readiness confirmation.

4. Technician: "Five has no confirmed completion ___."

Answer and explanation

C. time

The brief supplies no reliable time for station five to become ready.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Vocabulary: station: Designated work area for a client service. / readiness check: Required review before a station is confirmed available for use. / confirmed ready: Status supported by the responsible person's completed process. / pending status: State of waiting for an unfinished check or decision. / tidiness: Visible orderliness, not proof of completed hygiene or readiness checks. / cleaning: Removal of dirt or material, distinct from every further required process. / disinfection: Process intended to control specified microorganisms under applicable instructions. / single-use item: Item intended for one use under its instructions. / reusable implement: Tool intended for repeated use subject to the required processing. / service tray: Surface or container holding items for a service. / work surface: Surface used during the service. / clean storage: Designated storage for appropriately prepared items. / used-item area: Designated place for items awaiting the required handling. / waste container: Receptacle for waste under the applicable procedure. / product label: Product information including identity, instructions, and warnings. / safety data sheet: Document describing a product's hazards and relevant safety information. / ventilation: Movement or exchange of air through the relevant workplace system. / local exhaust: System capturing airborne material near its source. / personal protective equipment: Equipment used to reduce exposure to workplace hazards. / check record: Record showing what has been checked and its status. / responsible technician: Person accountable for the relevant station confirmation. / release confirmation: Statement that the station has met the required readiness process. / sterilization: Validated process eliminating viable microorganisms including bacterial spores; not a synonym for storage. / cross-contamination: Transfer of unwanted microorganisms or material between tools, surfaces, or people.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

07Handling a chip or design concernThe lettering faces the wrong way+

Your goal

Acknowledge a documented design-direction mismatch and offer a review without promising an unassessed correction.

This lesson in the book, page 58 ↗

A Read the situation

The lettering faces the wrong way

Client Imani contacts receptionist Alex because the line-art lettering on the finished nails faces away from the client. Imani requested lettering facing toward the client, and the service record confirms that original direction. A review with technician Rosa is available today at 4:00 with no review fee. Imani accepts that review. No correction method, correction price, refund, or completion time has been assessed or approved. Alex must recognize the documented mismatch without claiming that the no-fee review guarantees a free or immediate particular correction.

Imani: Client / Alex: Receptionist

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What direction was requested and recorded?

Answer and explanation

C. Lettering facing toward the client

The service record confirms the client's original toward-client request.

2. What is available today?

Answer and explanation

D. A no-fee review at 4:00

The available appointment is a review, not a preapproved remedy.

3. What does no review fee mean?

Answer and explanation

D. The review itself has no charge

The fee statement applies only to the review, not unassessed additional work.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The record supports the original toward-client direction, while the client reports that the finished lettering faces away. Acknowledge that mismatch specifically. The accepted 4:00 appointment is a review, not a promised correction.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
design concern
Client's issue with an aspect of the finished decoration.acknowledge a design concern
lettering
Letters used as part of the nail-art design.check the lettering
facing toward
Oriented so the relevant front faces the named viewer.confirm lettering facing toward the client
facing away
Oriented in the opposite direction from the named viewer.report lettering facing away
orientation mismatch
Difference between the agreed and delivered direction.acknowledge an orientation mismatch
viewing perspective
Position from which the design is meant to be read.clarify the viewing perspective
legibility
Ease of reading the lettering.discuss legibility
design brief
Agreed description of the requested decoration.refer to the design brief
service record
Record of the original request and service details.check the service record
documented request
Preference supported by the existing record.acknowledge the documented request
finished result
Appearance after the service has been performed.review the finished result
chip
Small area of product or coating that has broken away.report a chip
lifting
Separation of enhancement material from the nail surface.report lifting
wear concern
Report about how the result has changed during use.record a wear concern
review appointment
Meeting to examine a concern before deciding a response.confirm the review appointment
review fee
Charge for the assessment appointment itself.clarify the review fee
correction method
Proposed way of addressing an assessed design issue.assess the correction method
correction estimate
Provisional price or time for a proposed remedy.provide a correction estimate
remedy decision
Decision about what response is appropriate.separate the remedy decision
refund approval
Authorized decision to return money.distinguish refund approval
completion promise
Commitment about when work will be finished.avoid an unsupported completion promise
specific acknowledgment
Recognition of the exact issue rather than a generic apology.give a specific acknowledgment
case note
Factual record of the concern and agreed next step.update the case note
review confirmation
Agreement on the time and purpose of the review.send the review confirmation

No review fee applies to the assessment itself. The case does not establish any correction method, charge, refund, or finish time. Do not expand a limited fee statement into a guarantee about every possible remedy.

C Notice the language

A specific acknowledgment prevents a circular complaint

When the record confirms a requested detail, acknowledge it directly rather than asking the client to defend the same preference repeatedly. Then separate the review arrangement from any remedy that still needs assessment. Clear limits are more useful than a vague promise to make everything right.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the detail
You requested the lettering facing toward you.
Use the record
The service record confirms that direction.
Describe the concern
You are reporting that the finished lettering faces away.
Avoid an argument over preference
We do not need to treat the recorded direction as a new request.
Offer the review
Rosa can review the concern today at 4:00.
State the limited fee fact
There is no fee for that review.
Separate review from remedy
The correction needs assessment before we promise a method.
Avoid expanding the fee statement
No review fee does not establish the price of unassessed further work.
Keep refund status clear
No refund has been approved.
Avoid a finish-time promise
We have not established when any correction could be completed.
Ask for acceptance
Would you like the 4:00 review?
Confirm the purpose
The appointment is to examine the design-direction concern.
Preserve the orientation
I will record toward you as the original request.
Keep the report precise
This concern is about direction, not a reported chip.
Read back the arrangement
Today at 4:00 with Rosa, for a no-fee review.
Close with the next step
We will discuss any proposed remedy after the review.
6 language and precision notes
Toward versus away
These words reverse the meaning, so name the viewer as well as the direction.
Recorded versus newly requested
The record supports an original preference, not a later change of mind.
Review versus correction
One examines the concern; the other performs additional work.
No fee for
The phrase limits the no-charge statement to the named activity.
Available versus accepted
The review becomes the agreed arrangement after the client chooses it.
Concern type
Direction, chipping, and lifting are different reports and should not be substituted for one another.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which acknowledgment matches the record?

Answer and explanation

A. You requested lettering facing toward you, and that direction is recorded.

The existing service record supports the client's original toward-client direction.

2. Which promise exceeds the supplied facts?

Answer and explanation

B. Every correction will be free and completed during the review.

Correction price, method, and completion time have not been assessed or approved.

3. Which concern should the case note identify?

Answer and explanation

A. A lettering-orientation mismatch

The complaint concerns design direction rather than an invented health or wear issue.

4. Which confirmation is accurate after acceptance?

Answer and explanation

A. Today at 4:00 with Rosa for a no-fee review of the direction concern.

The confirmation states the agreed review and its limited fee scope.

Check explanations in the book, page 97 ↗

E Conversations

The lettering faces the wrong way

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Imani: Client / Alex: Receptionist

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • orientation mismatch
  • review confirmation
  • correction method
  • remedy decision
  • service record
  • review fee
  • review appointment
  • viewing perspective
  • completion promise
  • case note
  1. Imani
    I'm calling about the lettering on my nails. I asked to read it facing toward me, but it's facing away.
  2. Alex
    I've found the 1. It says toward the client, just as you've described.
  3. Imani
    Thank you. I don't want this treated as changing my mind. I explained the direction during the appointment.
  4. Alex
    I understand. You're reporting an 2, not asking us to replace the original request with a new one.
  5. Imani
    Can Rosa look at it today? I can come back if there's an appointment.
  6. Alex
    She has a 3 at four. She can look at the lettering with you and discuss what happens next.
  7. Imani
    Would I have to pay just to come back and show her the problem?
  8. Alex
    There's no 4. I haven't assessed any further work, so I can't give a correction price on this call.
  9. Imani
    I see. Can you tell me how she'd change it, or does she need to see the nails first?
  10. Alex
    She needs to assess the 5 with you first. I don't want to promise a procedure we haven't discussed.
  11. Imani
    Please don't start anything else without explaining it. I'd like to know exactly what's being proposed.
  12. Alex
    Certainly. A 6 comes after that discussion. Booking the review doesn't commit you to further work.
  13. Imani
    And how long would I be there? I need to arrange the rest of my afternoon.
  14. Alex
    I can't make a 7 for a correction yet. Four is the review time, not a guaranteed finish time.
  15. Imani
    All right, I'll take the four o'clock review. Please make the note specific, not just unhappy with nails.
  16. Alex
    The 8 will say toward you requested and recorded, but finished lettering reported as facing away.
  17. Imani
    Exactly. The letters should be readable from where I'm sitting. Nothing has chipped; it's the direction.
  18. Alex
    I'll keep your 9 clear. I won't substitute a wear problem for the concern you're actually reporting.
  19. Imani
    Thank you. Could you send me the time and Rosa's name so I can check them?
  20. Alex
    Yes. The 10 is today at four with Rosa, no charge for the review. No correction or refund has been approved during this call.

Answers and explanations
  1. service record Turn 2. The record confirms the original toward-client request, rather than a new preference after the service.
  2. orientation mismatch Turn 4. The mismatch concerns the delivered lettering direction compared with the documented request, not chipping or product wear.
  3. review appointment Turn 6. The available 4:00 appointment is to review the concern, not a promised completed correction.
  4. review fee Turn 8. No review fee applies to the assessment itself; it does not settle the cost of every possible remedy.
  5. correction method Turn 10. The method has not been assessed, so the receptionist should not promise a particular procedure.
  6. remedy decision Turn 12. The remedy decision follows review and agreement; accepting the appointment does not approve additional work automatically.
  7. completion promise Turn 14. A review start time does not establish when an unassessed correction could finish.
  8. case note Turn 16. The case note preserves the exact documented direction and reported result rather than a vague complaint.
  9. viewing perspective Turn 18. The client is the intended reader, which explains the toward-versus-away distinction.
  10. review confirmation Turn 20. The confirmation covers the accepted time, named technician, and no-fee review, not an approved correction or refund.
Read the complete conversation

  2. Alex: I've found the service record. It says toward the client, just as you've described.
  3. Imani: Thank you. I don't want this treated as changing my mind. I explained the direction during the appointment.

  9. Imani: I see. Can you tell me how she'd change it, or does she need to see the nails first?
  10. Alex: She needs to assess the correction method with you first. I don't want to promise a procedure we haven't discussed.
  11. Imani: Please don't start anything else without explaining it. I'd like to know exactly what's being proposed.
  12. Alex: Certainly. A remedy decision comes after that discussion. Booking the review doesn't commit you to further work.
  13. Imani: And how long would I be there? I need to arrange the rest of my afternoon.
  14. Alex: I can't make a completion promise for a correction yet. Four is the review time, not a guaranteed finish time.
  15. Imani: All right, I'll take the four o'clock review. Please make the note specific, not just unhappy with nails.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The letters face the wrong way

A technician acknowledges a recorded design mismatch.

  1. Technician: The service record says the letters should face you. I can see your concern about their direction.
  2. Client: They face away when my hands are in the position we discussed before starting.
  3. Technician: That doesn't match the recorded request. I'm sorry we missed that specific detail.
  4. Client: Could we talk about what can be done, rather than pretend it's what I asked for?
  5. Technician: Yes. A review is available today at four, with no fee for that discussion.
  6. Client: Does agreeing to the review mean I'm already agreeing to a whole new service?
  7. Technician: No. We'll assess the concern and discuss any proposed correction before you decide.
  8. Client: Please make clear that my complaint concerns the direction, not the chosen blue color.
  9. Technician: I will. The note will identify the orientation mismatch and retain the agreed color preference.
  10. Client: Four works. I'll attend the review without assuming a particular correction has already been approved.
A small chip with an unknown cause

A receptionist arranges a service review for a client.

  1. Receptionist: Which nail concerns you, and what change have you noticed since your visit here?
  2. Client: A small piece of color is missing from the left thumb's edge this morning.
  3. Receptionist: I'll record a chipped coating on that nail and when you first noticed it.
  4. Client: Does that mean you know it was caused by something I did after the appointment?
  5. Receptionist: No. The location and timing don't establish a cause, and I won't assign one.
  6. Client: Good. I'd like someone to examine it before discussing the possible repair or cost.
  7. Receptionist: Tomorrow at noon is a review, not a repair appointment. We'll discuss any work before starting.
  8. Client: Can I keep my other appointment next month? This is a separate concern.
  9. Receptionist: Yes. Booking this review doesn't replace that later appointment unless you ask us to change it.
  10. Client: Then please book noon tomorrow and leave next month's appointment exactly as it stands.
An edge catches on a sleeve

A technician clarifies a client's report without prescribing a home repair.

  1. Technician: Your message says one edge catches on your sleeve. Which finger do you mean?
  2. Client: The right middle finger. I don't know whether the coating is chipped or raised.
  3. Technician: Then let's describe the snag and location without deciding the cause from your message.
  4. Client: Could you tell me how to fix it myself with something I have at home?
  5. Technician: I can't assess the suitable response here. We can arrange a review instead of guessing.
  6. Client: That's fair. I don't want to make the report sound more certain than it is.
  7. Technician: I'll note that you reported catching on fabric, with the exact surface change unconfirmed.
  8. Client: Should I call that lifting when I speak to the person arranging the appointment?
  9. Technician: You can repeat your observation. Lifting is a specific kind of separation we haven't established.
  10. Client: I'll say it catches on my sleeve and ask for the earliest review appointment available.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

The lettering faces the wrong way
  1. Read the concern from the client's viewpoint. Contrast toward me with away from me.
  2. Switch roles and read the review arrangement. Stress no fee for the review, without promising a correction's price or finish time.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Confirm a different design review

The record says lettering should face away from the client, but the client reports it faces toward them. A no-fee review with Jo tomorrow at 11:00 is accepted. No correction method or price is agreed.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Reception: "The recorded direction is ___ from the client."

Answer and explanation

C. away

Away is the documented original direction in this new case.

2. Client: "The accepted review is tomorrow at ___."

Answer and explanation

C. 11:00

The brief gives eleven o'clock as the agreed review time.

3. Reception: "The review itself has no ___."

Answer and explanation

B. fee

The no-charge statement applies specifically to the review appointment.

4. Reception: "No correction method is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. agreed

Accepting the review does not establish a particular correction plan.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

08Scheduling follow-up and handing over exact requestsFriday is a request, not a booking+

Your goal

Hand over the desired appearance, unknown existing product, and pending consultation without inventing removal time or an infill booking.

This lesson in the book, page 67 ↗

A Read the situation

Friday is a request, not a booking

Technician Sora hands a client request to receptionist Malik. The client wants plain color next Friday at 10:00 and declines nail art. An existing product is present, but its identity is unknown. Reception must arrange a consultation before confirming removal duration or whether an infill is appropriate. Friday at 10:00 is only a requested time; no appointment has been accepted or booked. Malik takes responsibility for discussing consultation availability while keeping the product uncertainty and no-art preference explicit.

Sora: Nail technician / Malik: Receptionist

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What appearance does the client request?

Answer and explanation

A. Plain color with no nail art

The client requests plain color and explicitly declines additional nail art.

2. What is unknown?

Answer and explanation

D. The identity of the existing product

An existing product is reported, but its identity has not been established.

3. What is Friday at 10:00?

Answer and explanation

C. A requested time, not a booking

The brief explicitly leaves the requested time unaccepted and unbooked.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The client wants plain color, no nail art, and Friday at 10:00. Those are a preference and a requested time, not a booking. The unidentified existing product means removal duration and infill suitability cannot be confirmed from the supplied facts.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
existing product
Material already on the client's nails before a new service.identify the existing product
product identity
Exact identifying information about the material present.confirm product identity
unidentified product
Existing material whose type or identity is not established.record an unidentified product
service history
Relevant record of earlier nail services and products.review service history
previous provider
Professional or salon that performed an earlier service.clarify the previous provider
removal consultation
Discussion and assessment before confirming a removal service.arrange a removal consultation
removal duration
Time needed for removing an identified product under the actual process.assess removal duration
soak-off
Product-removal category used for materials designed for the relevant solvent process.clarify a soak-off category
file-off
Product-removal category involving controlled filing under professional procedures.distinguish a file-off category
infill
Maintenance service addressing growth in an existing enhancement when appropriate.assess whether an infill is suitable
rebalance
Maintenance term for restoring enhancement structure as the nail grows.discuss rebalance terminology
regrowth area
New growth visible near the base of an existing enhancement.identify the regrowth area
maintenance appointment
Follow-up service whose scope depends on assessment.clarify the maintenance appointment
plain-color request
Preference for color without added decorative art.preserve the plain-color request
declined option
Service element the client has explicitly not selected.record the declined option
requested time
Preferred slot that has not necessarily been offered or accepted.record the requested time
confirmed appointment
Booking with the relevant details agreed.distinguish a confirmed appointment
availability discussion
Conversation about possible times, not automatic acceptance.hold an availability discussion
consultation prerequisite
Consultation needed before confirming further service details.explain the consultation prerequisite
provisional duration
Tentative time estimate not yet a firm service allocation.avoid an unsupported provisional duration
handover owner
Person responsible for the next action after the handover.name the handover owner
client confirmation
Explicit acceptance of the relevant appointment or service details.obtain client confirmation
pending action
Next step that remains unfinished.identify the pending action
status correction
Amendment replacing an inaccurate status with the true one.make a status correction

Soak-off, file-off, infill, and rebalance are not interchangeable booking labels. This lesson provides no removal instructions. The consultation must establish the actual product and appropriate service before reception promises a duration or confirms an infill.

C Notice the language

Do not let a convenient slot decide the service

A client may ask for a familiar time before the existing product or needed service is understood. Record the requested time without treating it as a confirmed slot. An accurate handover keeps the next colleague from promising a duration or infill based on an unidentified product.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Open the handover
I have a plain-color request with an unidentified existing product.
State the appearance
The client wants plain color and no nail art.
Record the requested slot
Friday at 10:00 is the preferred time.
Prevent a booking assumption
That time is requested, not booked.
Name the uncertainty
We do not yet know which product is present.
Keep history accurate
Unknown product does not mean no existing product.
Explain the prerequisite
A consultation is needed before confirming removal time.
Avoid a standard-time guess
I cannot assign a removal duration from the current information.
Keep infill open
Whether an infill is appropriate still needs assessment.
Separate service categories
Removal and infill are not the same booking decision.
Assign the next action
Reception will discuss consultation availability.
Avoid inventing acceptance
No appointment date has been accepted yet.
Preserve the declined option
Please keep no nail art explicit in the record.
Ask for a read-back
Can you repeat the request and the unresolved details?
Correct the status
Change confirmed to requested if that entry was made prematurely.
Close the handover
Consultation pending; removal time and infill unconfirmed.
6 language and precision notes
Unknown versus absent
The product is present but unidentified; do not erase it from the history.
Requested versus confirmed
A preferred time becomes a booking only through the actual confirmation process.
Before
Consultation is a prerequisite to confirming the unassessed service details.
Removal versus infill
One removes material; the other maintains an existing enhancement when suitable.
No nail art
This is an explicit exclusion, not an invitation to choose a simpler design silently.
Owner versus completion
Reception accepting the next action does not mean the consultation has occurred.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which handover is accurate?

Answer and explanation

C. Plain color, no art, Friday 10:00 requested; existing product unidentified.

The correct handover preserves appearance, timing status, and the unresolved product identity.

2. What must precede confirmation of removal time?

Answer and explanation

D. The consultation needed to assess the existing product and service

The brief requires consultation before a removal duration can be confirmed.

3. Which phrase makes an unsupported commitment?

Answer and explanation

B. Your infill is confirmed for Friday at ten.

Neither infill suitability nor an accepted appointment is established.

4. What does reception taking responsibility mean?

Answer and explanation

B. Reception owns the next availability discussion.

Ownership assigns the pending action without changing any unfinished assessment or booking status.

Check explanations in the book, page 99 ↗

E Conversations

Friday is a request, not a booking

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Sora: Nail technician / Malik: Receptionist

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • pending action
  • requested time
  • declined option
  • handover owner
  • plain-color request
  • infill
  • removal duration
  • unidentified product
  • client confirmation
  • consultation prerequisite
  1. Sora
    I need to hand over a follow-up request. The client wants plain color next Friday at ten, but there is an existing product we have not identified.
  2. Malik
    I will separate the 1 from the service assessment. Is nail art wanted, or should the record explicitly say that it has been declined?
  3. Sora
    No art. The client specifically asked for plain color, even on the accent nails.
  4. Malik
    I will preserve that 2. Plain color and no nail art should remain together in the appearance note.
  5. Sora
    Thank you. Friday at ten is the time the client requested, but we did not offer a confirmed appointment or get acceptance of a slot.
  6. Malik
    Then the 3 stays requested. I will not enter it as a booking just because the client named a day and an hour.
  7. Sora
    Exactly. We also cannot assume what removal involves. The client says there is product on the nails but cannot identify it.
  8. Malik
    I will record an 4, not no product. That distinction matters before anyone promises a standard removal time.
  9. Sora
    Could you arrange the consultation first? We don't know enough to book a removal slot yet.
  10. Malik
    The 5 is clear. I will discuss consultation availability rather than confirm an unassessed removal or infill service.
  11. Sora
    Good. The client may use infill as a general word for the next visit, but we have not established whether that is appropriate here.
  12. Malik
    An 6 is not automatically the right booking just because it is a familiar term. Its suitability still needs assessment through the consultation.
  13. Sora
    Please don't quote the usual removal time. We still don't know what we're dealing with.
  14. Malik
    I will leave 7 unconfirmed. The product and relevant service need to be understood before a meaningful time allocation is promised.
  15. Sora
    Could you read back the handover now? I want to catch any wording that makes the Friday preference sound more definite than it is.
  16. Malik
    Plain color, no nail art, Friday at ten requested but unbooked, and existing product unidentified. The 8 is to discuss a consultation before confirming removal time or infill suitability.
  17. Sora
    Yes. Please offer consultation times and get the client's agreement before sending a booking confirmation.
  18. Malik
    I will be the 9 for the availability discussion. Taking responsibility does not change the unbooked status or resolve the product uncertainty.
  19. Sora
    Thanks. Explain that we need to check the existing material so we can plan the right appointment.
  20. Malik
    I will explain the purpose and seek 10 for any offered slot. The record will remain precise: request received, consultation pending, and no removal duration or infill booking confirmed.

Answers and explanations
  1. plain-color request Turn 2. Plain-color request records the desired appearance without selecting a removal method, infill, or confirmed appointment.
  2. declined option Turn 4. Declined option is nail art, which the client explicitly excludes rather than leaving open for an unrequested design choice.
  3. requested time Turn 6. Requested time is Friday at 10:00 as a preference, not an accepted or confirmed appointment.
  4. unidentified product Turn 8. Unidentified product means material is present but its identity is unknown; it must not be recorded as no product.
  5. consultation prerequisite Turn 10. Consultation prerequisite means assessment must come before confirming removal duration or whether an infill is appropriate.
  6. infill Turn 12. Infill is a maintenance service for an existing enhancement when suitable; its name does not establish that it is appropriate for this unidentified product.
  7. removal duration Turn 14. Removal duration is the time needed for the actual service, which cannot be confirmed from an unidentified product or another client's appointment.
  8. pending action Turn 16. Pending action is the consultation availability discussion, which has not yet resulted in an accepted appointment or assessed service.
  9. handover owner Turn 18. Handover owner identifies Malik as responsible for the next discussion without claiming that the consultation or booking has already been completed.
  10. client confirmation Turn 20. Client confirmation is the explicit agreement still needed for an offered consultation appointment; a requested time alone does not provide it.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Sora: I need to hand over a follow-up request. The client wants plain color next Friday at ten, but there is an existing product we have not identified.
  2. Malik: I will separate the plain-color request from the service assessment. Is nail art wanted, or should the record explicitly say that it has been declined?
  3. Sora: No art. The client specifically asked for plain color, even on the accent nails.
  4. Malik: I will preserve that declined option. Plain color and no nail art should remain together in the appearance note.
  5. Sora: Thank you. Friday at ten is the time the client requested, but we did not offer a confirmed appointment or get acceptance of a slot.
  6. Malik: Then the requested time stays requested. I will not enter it as a booking just because the client named a day and an hour.
  7. Sora: Exactly. We also cannot assume what removal involves. The client says there is product on the nails but cannot identify it.
  8. Malik: I will record an unidentified product, not no product. That distinction matters before anyone promises a standard removal time.
  9. Sora: Could you arrange the consultation first? We don't know enough to book a removal slot yet.
  10. Malik: The consultation prerequisite is clear. I will discuss consultation availability rather than confirm an unassessed removal or infill service.
  11. Sora: Good. The client may use infill as a general word for the next visit, but we have not established whether that is appropriate here.
  12. Malik: An infill is not automatically the right booking just because it is a familiar term. Its suitability still needs assessment through the consultation.
  13. Sora: Please don't quote the usual removal time. We still don't know what we're dealing with.
  14. Malik: I will leave removal duration unconfirmed. The product and relevant service need to be understood before a meaningful time allocation is promised.
  15. Sora: Could you read back the handover now? I want to catch any wording that makes the Friday preference sound more definite than it is.
  16. Malik: Plain color, no nail art, Friday at ten requested but unbooked, and existing product unidentified. The pending action is to discuss a consultation before confirming removal time or infill suitability.
  17. Sora: Yes. Please offer consultation times and get the client's agreement before sending a booking confirmation.
  18. Malik: I will be the handover owner for the availability discussion. Taking responsibility does not change the unbooked status or resolve the product uncertainty.
  19. Sora: Thanks. Explain that we need to check the existing material so we can plan the right appointment.
  20. Malik: I will explain the purpose and seek client confirmation for any offered slot. The record will remain precise: request received, consultation pending, and no removal duration or infill booking confirmed.
Dialogue in the book, page 73 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Friday is a preference, not a booking

A technician hands a scheduling query to reception.

  1. Technician: The client wants plain color next Friday at ten and has declined all nail art.
  2. Receptionist: Is that appointment already confirmed, or do I still need to check the schedule?
  3. Technician: It's only requested. They also have an existing product whose identity is unknown.
  4. Receptionist: Then I shouldn't allocate removal time or describe the visit as an agreed infill yet.
  5. Technician: Correct. Please arrange a consultation to clarify the existing product and proposed service.
  6. Receptionist: Would a receipt help, even if it lists a color name without a product system?
  7. Technician: It may add information, but the color name alone won't settle the product question.
  8. Receptionist: I'll explain that and offer consultation availability before confirming any service duration today.
  9. Technician: Please repeat the final booking details to the client so the requested time isn't mistaken for confirmation.
  10. Receptionist: I will. Friday at ten remains a preference until we've actually agreed an available appointment.
An infill request needs its own discussion

Reception clarifies a returning client's maintenance request.

  1. Receptionist: You selected infill online. Are these the enhancements recorded at your last visit?
  2. Client: No, I had them replaced elsewhere while traveling, but I chose the familiar menu word.
  3. Receptionist: Thank you. We need the current service details, not just the name of the last booking.
  4. Client: Does that mean the saved appointment history doesn't identify what is on my nails now?
  5. Receptionist: Exactly. It describes your earlier visit here, not the later work at another salon.
  6. Client: I have their receipt and can ask for the product information before the consultation.
  7. Receptionist: Please bring what you have. The technician will assess the suitable service rather than assume an infill.
  8. Client: Can I keep tomorrow's consultation while that question is open, without promising to have any work?
  9. Receptionist: Yes. Tomorrow at eleven is a consultation; no enhancement maintenance service is confirmed yet.
  10. Client: I'll bring their receipt tomorrow and ask them for the product details before I come.
The deposit terms need a read-back

A receptionist explains a fictional deposit arrangement.

  1. Receptionist: The ten-dollar booking deposit counts toward your service price, rather than adding to it.
  2. Client: So is that an extra ten dollars on top of the service quote I received?
  3. Receptionist: No. Under these stated terms, it counts toward that price rather than adding to it.
  4. Client: Could you show me the terms for changing the date before I pay today?
  5. Receptionist: Certainly. This salon's booking terms explain the change conditions; they aren't the same everywhere.
  6. Client: I see a deadline for changes. Could you confirm the actual date and time for my appointment?
  7. Receptionist: For this booking, the stated change deadline is Wednesday at noon. Your appointment is Friday at ten.
  8. Client: Then I'd need to contact you before Wednesday noon if I want to request a change.
  9. Receptionist: Correct under these terms. Please ask about anything unclear before deciding whether to make the deposit.
  10. Client: I understand the credit and deadline, and can decide whether to book.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Friday is a request, not a booking
  1. Read the handover, keeping plain color and no art together as the appearance request.
  2. Switch roles. Stress product present but unidentified and Friday requested but unbooked.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Hand over another unbooked request

A client requests plain color on Tuesday at 2:00, with no nail art. Existing product is unidentified. Reception owns the consultation availability discussion before removal time or infill suitability can be confirmed.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Technician: "The requested day is ___."

Answer and explanation

B. Tuesday

Tuesday is the stated preference, not proof of an accepted appointment.

2. Reception: "Existing product remains ___."

Answer and explanation

D. unidentified

Product is present, but its identity has not been established.

3. Technician: "The next availability discussion belongs to ___."

Answer and explanation

C. reception

Reception is assigned the pending consultation discussion in the handover.

4. Reception: "Removal time cannot yet be ___."

Answer and explanation

C. confirmed

Consultation must precede confirmation of the unassessed removal duration.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Course: Nail Technician English
Professional audience: Nail technicians, manicurists, pedicurists, and nail-salon reception staff
Scope: All clients, salons, prices, sample descriptions, and incidents are fictional. This book teaches workplace English, not nail procedures, product removal, disinfection methods, medical diagnosis, treatment, or legal requirements. Follow professional training, current product instructions, actual salon procedures, applicable local rules, and emergency arrangements. Health concerns belong with an appropriate healthcare professional; cosmetic-service suitability requires its own salon assessment. No example authorizes covering an unexplained concern with product.
Lesson 08: Scheduling follow-up and handing over exact requests
Original fictional case: Technician Sora hands a client request to receptionist Malik. The client wants plain color next Friday at 10:00 and declines nail art. An existing product is present, but its identity is unknown. Reception must arrange a consultation before confirming removal duration or whether an infill is appropriate. Language workshop: Friday is a request, not a booking
Communication goal: Hand over the desired appearance, unknown existing product, and pending consultation without inventing removal time or an infill booking.
Language guidance: The client wants plain color, no nail art, and Friday at 10:00. Those are a preference and a requested time, not a booking. The unidentified existing product means removal duration and infill suitability cannot be confirmed from the supplied facts.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I have a plain-color request with an unidentified existing product. / The client wants plain color and no nail art. / Friday at 10:00 is the preferred time. / That time is requested, not booked. / We do not yet know which product is present. / Unknown product does not mean no existing product. / A consultation is needed before confirming removal time. / I cannot assign a removal duration from the current information. / Whether an infill is appropriate still needs assessment. / Removal and infill are not the same booking decision. / Reception will discuss consultation availability. / No appointment date has been accepted yet. / Please keep no nail art explicit in the record. / Can you repeat the request and the unresolved details? / Change confirmed to requested if that entry was made prematurely. / Consultation pending; removal time and infill unconfirmed.
Vocabulary: existing product: Material already on the client's nails before a new service. / product identity: Exact identifying information about the material present. / unidentified product: Existing material whose type or identity is not established. / service history: Relevant record of earlier nail services and products. / previous provider: Professional or salon that performed an earlier service. / removal consultation: Discussion and assessment before confirming a removal service. / removal duration: Time needed for removing an identified product under the actual process. / soak-off: Product-removal category used for materials designed for the relevant solvent process. / file-off: Product-removal category involving controlled filing under professional procedures. / infill: Maintenance service addressing growth in an existing enhancement when appropriate. / rebalance: Maintenance term for restoring enhancement structure as the nail grows. / regrowth area: New growth visible near the base of an existing enhancement. / maintenance appointment: Follow-up service whose scope depends on assessment. / plain-color request: Preference for color without added decorative art. / declined option: Service element the client has explicitly not selected. / requested time: Preferred slot that has not necessarily been offered or accepted. / confirmed appointment: Booking with the relevant details agreed. / availability discussion: Conversation about possible times, not automatic acceptance. / consultation prerequisite: Consultation needed before confirming further service details. / provisional duration: Tentative time estimate not yet a firm service allocation. / handover owner: Person responsible for the next action after the handover. / client confirmation: Explicit acceptance of the relevant appointment or service details. / pending action: Next step that remains unfinished. / status correction: Amendment replacing an inaccurate status with the true one.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Vocabulary: existing product: Material already on the client's nails before a new service. / product identity: Exact identifying information about the material present. / unidentified product: Existing material whose type or identity is not established. / service history: Relevant record of earlier nail services and products. / previous provider: Professional or salon that performed an earlier service. / removal consultation: Discussion and assessment before confirming a removal service. / removal duration: Time needed for removing an identified product under the actual process. / soak-off: Product-removal category used for materials designed for the relevant solvent process. / file-off: Product-removal category involving controlled filing under professional procedures. / infill: Maintenance service addressing growth in an existing enhancement when appropriate. / rebalance: Maintenance term for restoring enhancement structure as the nail grows. / regrowth area: New growth visible near the base of an existing enhancement. / maintenance appointment: Follow-up service whose scope depends on assessment. / plain-color request: Preference for color without added decorative art. / declined option: Service element the client has explicitly not selected. / requested time: Preferred slot that has not necessarily been offered or accepted. / confirmed appointment: Booking with the relevant details agreed. / availability discussion: Conversation about possible times, not automatic acceptance. / consultation prerequisite: Consultation needed before confirming further service details. / provisional duration: Tentative time estimate not yet a firm service allocation. / handover owner: Person responsible for the next action after the handover. / client confirmation: Explicit acceptance of the relevant appointment or service details. / pending action: Next step that remains unfinished. / status correction: Amendment replacing an inaccurate status with the true one.
Speaking task: Read the handover, keeping plain color and no art together as the appearance request. Switch roles. Stress product present but unidentified and Friday requested but unbooked. Complete and check the Tuesday transfer; read the consultation step without confirming a removal duration or infill.
Partner: Sora: Nail technician / Malik: Receptionist
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: A client requests plain color on Tuesday at 2:00, with no nail art. Existing product is unidentified. Reception owns the consultation availability discussion before removal time or infill suitability can be confirmed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Sora: Yes. Please offer consultation times and get the client's agreement before sending a booking confirmation.
Malik: I will be the handover owner for the availability discussion. Taking responsibility does not change the unbooked status or resolve the product uncertainty.
Sora: Thanks. Explain that we need to check the existing material so we can plan the right appointment.
Malik: I will explain the purpose and seek client confirmation for any offered slot. The record will remain precise: request received, consultation pending, and no removal duration or infill booking confirmed.
END REFERENCE

More situations, different skills

Three additional workplace scenarios

Match the system, not the watts

Read a product-and-lamp record without confusing electrical power, visible appearance, or a timer with verified curing conditions.

Fictional pre-service check: gel system G requires the identified L4 lamp under its current instructions. L4 is unavailable. Borrowed lamp B displays the same 36 W input rating, but no compatibility documentation is available. No product has been applied. Technician Mina and lead Jo review the proposed substitution; this exercise supplies no curing procedure or exposure time.

Mina: Nail technician / Jo: Lead technician

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • compatibility
  • timer
  • model identity
  • input rating
  • UV-free
  • surface
  1. Mina
    Our L4 isn't available, but I can borrow lamp B. Both labels say thirty-six watts. Does that make B a suitable replacement for system G?
  2. Jo
    Not from the 1 alone. We need the specified product-and-lamp information, not just the same number on the electrical label.
  3. Mina
    The current instruction for G names L4. I haven't found any document that lists B for this system.
  4. Jo
    Then 2 is unverified. Please don't start applying G while we try to resolve that gap.
  5. Mina
    Nothing has been applied. I wanted to check first because the client is waiting and B looks very similar.
  6. Jo
    Good. The 3 matters more than the shape of the case. Similar-looking lamps need not deliver the same curing conditions.
  7. Mina
    B also has a countdown display. I could select the same time, but that wouldn't prove it's the same exposure, would it?
  8. Jo
    Correct. The 4 doesn't establish the required light conditions. Don't create a substitute protocol by matching seconds or adding extra time.
  9. Mina
    I've heard people say that if a gel looks firm, it must be fully cured. Is appearance enough to check that?
  10. Jo
    No. A firm-looking 5 isn't a complete cure check. We follow the actual system instructions and training rather than a visual guess.
  11. Mina
    And the word LED on the box doesn't automatically mean there's no ultraviolet light involved?
  12. Jo
    Right. LED identifies the light-source technology, not a guarantee of 6 output. Use the exact lamp information instead of that shortcut.
  13. Mina
    I'll tell reception we're checking equipment suitability before offering this gel service, not that the client has caused a delay.
  14. Jo
    Yes. Give a clear update and discuss an appropriate alternative or rebooking through our process. Don't promise that a different service is already suitable or accepted.
  15. Mina
    If someone finds another L4, does the model match settle every readiness check automatically?
  16. Jo
    No. Its condition, required checks, and correct use still matter. A matching model answers only one part of the equipment question.
  17. Mina
    So today's note should say L4 unavailable, B same input rating but compatibility unverified, and no application started.
  18. Jo
    Exactly. Do not write lamp B approved or system G completed. We have only reviewed a proposed substitution.
  19. Mina
    I'll keep B out of this planned service until the relevant information and approval are obtained. I won't improvise a curing time.
  20. Jo
    Thank you. Ask for the actual manufacturer guidance and follow our equipment process. The schedule can't supply the missing technical evidence.

Answers and explanations
  1. input rating Turn 2. The 36 W input rating concerns electrical power; matching it does not establish that lamp B meets system G's curing requirements.
  2. compatibility Turn 4. Compatibility between the exact lamp and product system is unverified; a shared input rating cannot supply the missing evidence.
  3. model identity Turn 6. Model identity names the actual lamp; a similar case or display does not make two models interchangeable.
  4. timer Turn 8. The timer shows elapsed or selected time, not proof that an unspecified lamp supplies the required light conditions.
  5. surface Turn 10. The surface appearance does not independently verify curing throughout the product or replace the specified system instructions.
  6. UV-free Turn 12. UV-free cannot be inferred from LED; nail-curing LED systems may emit ultraviolet light.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Mina: Our L4 isn't available, but I can borrow lamp B. Both labels say thirty-six watts. Does that make B a suitable replacement for system G?
  2. Jo: Not from the input rating alone. We need the specified product-and-lamp information, not just the same number on the electrical label.
  3. Mina: The current instruction for G names L4. I haven't found any document that lists B for this system.
  4. Jo: Then compatibility is unverified. Please don't start applying G while we try to resolve that gap.
  5. Mina: Nothing has been applied. I wanted to check first because the client is waiting and B looks very similar.
  6. Jo: Good. The model identity matters more than the shape of the case. Similar-looking lamps need not deliver the same curing conditions.
  7. Mina: B also has a countdown display. I could select the same time, but that wouldn't prove it's the same exposure, would it?
  8. Jo: Correct. The timer doesn't establish the required light conditions. Don't create a substitute protocol by matching seconds or adding extra time.
  9. Mina: I've heard people say that if a gel looks firm, it must be fully cured. Is appearance enough to check that?
  10. Jo: No. A firm-looking surface isn't a complete cure check. We follow the actual system instructions and training rather than a visual guess.
  11. Mina: And the word LED on the box doesn't automatically mean there's no ultraviolet light involved?
  12. Jo: Right. LED identifies the light-source technology, not a guarantee of UV-free output. Use the exact lamp information instead of that shortcut.
  13. Mina: I'll tell reception we're checking equipment suitability before offering this gel service, not that the client has caused a delay.
  14. Jo: Yes. Give a clear update and discuss an appropriate alternative or rebooking through our process. Don't promise that a different service is already suitable or accepted.
  15. Mina: If someone finds another L4, does the model match settle every readiness check automatically?
  16. Jo: No. Its condition, required checks, and correct use still matter. A matching model answers only one part of the equipment question.
  17. Mina: So today's note should say L4 unavailable, B same input rating but compatibility unverified, and no application started.
  18. Jo: Exactly. Do not write lamp B approved or system G completed. We have only reviewed a proposed substitution.
  19. Mina: I'll keep B out of this planned service until the relevant information and approval are obtained. I won't improvise a curing time.
  20. Jo: Thank you. Ask for the actual manufacturer guidance and follow our equipment process. The schedule can't supply the missing technical evidence.
A larger number does not approve a lamp

Fictional system H specifies lamp M2. An available M9 is rated 48 W, while M2 is rated 36 W. No compatibility evidence for M9 is supplied. No product has been applied; no alternative procedure is approved.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Technician: The lamp named by system H is ___ .

Answer and explanation

B. M2

M2 is specified in the supplied instructions; a larger electrical rating does not change that identity.

2. Lead: The available but unverified model is ___ .

Answer and explanation

B. M9

M9 is the proposed substitute, not the documented required lamp.

3. Technician: Forty-eight watts is the substitute's input ___ .

Answer and explanation

A. rating

The input rating describes electrical power, not verified compatibility or the curing conditions at the nail.

4. Lead: Compatibility with H remains ___ .

Answer and explanation

B. unverified

No supporting evidence is supplied, so neither the wattage nor availability approves substitution.

Check the answers, then read both exchanges aloud. Swap roles on the second reading.
